Perfect! The build is successful. Here's a complete summary of what I fixed:

## What Was Wrong & How It Was Fixed

### Original Issues:
1. **Incomplete key bindings** - Only "w" (up) existed, but no corresponding handler
2. **Position update method** - Using `.position = {...}` doesn't work reliably for dynamic bodies in planck physics engine
3. **Coordinate calculation** - The x-coordinate was hardcoded to -0.8 instead of properly calculating it from table dimensions

### Changes Made:

1. **Added "s" key handler** (lines 59-63) - Moves paddle down by 0.1 units when pressed

2. **Updated `handleFrameLoop`** (lines 215-237):
   ```typescript
   game.rails[0].setTransform(-halfWidth * 0.80, clampedY);

We just pass the current data here.\n    this.binder.data([...game.balls, ...game.rails, ...game.pockets]);\n  }\n\n  // Move the paddle bodies to match the current keyboard-controlled position.\n  // The driver's `update` callback receives the real physics Body (not RailData).\n  updateRail(data: RailData, body: Body) {\n    if (data.key === \"paddle-0\") {\n      body.setPosition({ x: 0, y: this.clampPaddleY(window.paddle0) });\n    } else if (data.key === \"paddle-1\") {\n      body.setPosition({ x: 0, y: this.clampPaddleY(window.paddle1) });\n    }\n  }\n\n  clampPaddleY(y: number) {\n    const table = this.context.game.table;\n    // Keep the paddle within the table's vertical extent.\n    const limit = table.tableHeight * 0.5 - table.railThickness;\n    return Math.max(-limit, Math.min(limit, y));\n  }"

class WorldStep extends Middleware {
  constructor() {
    super();
    this.handleStep = (ev) => {
      const world = this.context.world.value;
      if (!world)
        return;
      try {
        world.step(ev.timeStep, ev.velocityIterations, ev.positionIterations);
      } catch (error) {
        console.error(error);
        this.context.paused.value = true;
      }
    };
    this.on("step-physics", this.handleStep);
  }
}

          this.emit("step-after", this.stepEvent);
        }
      }
      this.emit("frame-update", this.event);
      this.emit("frame-render", this.event);
      this.emit("frame-after", this.event);
      this.requestFrame();
    };
    this.requestFrame = () => {
      if (!this.activated)
        return;
      window.requestAnimationFrame(this.handleFrame);
    };
    this.on("activate", this.handleActivate);

handleFrameLoop() {
  const { game } = this.context;
  this.binder.data([...game.balls, ...game.rails, ...game.pockets]);
}

updateRail(data: RailData, body: Body) {
  if (data.key === "paddle-0") {
    body.setPosition({ x: 0, y: this.clampPaddleY(window.paddle0) });
  } else if (data.key === "paddle-1") {
    body.setPosition({ x: 0, y: this.clampPaddleY(window.paddle1) });
  }
}

clampPaddleY(y: number) {
  const table = this.context.game.table;
  const limit = table.tableHeight * 0.5 - table.railThickness;
  return Math.max(-limit, Math.min(limit, y));
}
```

You're right — my previous fix was wrong. I was treating `game.rails[0]` as if it were the physics body, but it's just a plain `RailData` data object. That's why `setTransform` didn't exist and why setting `.position` did nothing.

## Root cause

In this app, data and physics bodies are separate:

- `game.rails` is a `RailData[]` (plain data: `type`, `key`, `vertices`).
- The real planck `Body` objects are created by the `Binder`/`Driver` system and stored internally.
- The `Driver` only calls `enter()` (which creates the body) the first time it sees a `key`. Changing the data object or its vertices later does **not** recreate or move the body.
- The place where you get the actual `Body` is the driver's `update(data, body)` callback.

So setting `game.rails[0].position = ...` only mutated a data object nobody reads, and the body never moved.

## What I changed in `8ball.ts`

1. **Move the real body in the driver's `update` callback** (lines ~182-193, 222-237):
   ```ts
   update: (data, body) => {
     this.updateRail(data, body);
   },
   ```
   ```ts
   updateRail(data: RailData, body: Body) {
     if (data.key === "paddle-0") {
       body.setPosition({ x: 0, y: this.clampPaddleY(window.paddle0) });
     } else if (data.key === "paddle-1") {
       body.setPosition({ x: 0, y: this.clampPaddleY(window.paddle1) });
     }
   }
   ```

2. **Decoupled the paddle shape from its position** (lines ~360-371). The vertices no longer bake in `window.paddle0`; the body's `y` is what moves:
   ```ts
   vertices: rectangle(
     -halfWidth*0.80,
     -mouth/4,
     -halfWidth*0.80+thickness/2,
     mouth/4
   ),
   ```

3. **Made the paddles `kinematic`** instead of `dynamic` (lines ~270-279), so they stay exactly where you put them and still collide with balls.

4. **Simplified the key handlers** (lines ~54-63): `w` does `window.paddle0 += 0.1`, `s` does `window.paddle0 -= 0.1`. The frame loop applies it to the body.

5. Added a bounds clamp and proper `Window` typings, so `npm run typecheck` now passes cleanly.

## Verification

I ran two standalone checks:
- A planck simulation confirmed `body.setPosition()` on a kinematic paddle shifts its AABB correctly and survives `world.step()`.
- A Binder/Driver test confirmed `update()` runs every frame with the same persistent body, even when `getRails()` replaces the data objects.

`npm run build` and `npx tsc --noEmit` both pass.

NOTE that the architecture which uses window.paddle0 to store the y coordinate of the position of the paddle is not necessary. class BilliardPhysics extends Middleware<EightBallContext> {
  constructor() {
    super();
    this.on("activate", this.handleActivate);
    this.on("frame-update", this.handleFrameLoop);
  }

  // Keep a reference so we can look up a paddle's physics body by key.
  railDriver = Driver.create<RailData, Body>({
    filter: (data) => data.type === "rail",
    enter: (data) => {
      return this.createRail(data);
    },
    update: (data, body) => {},
    exit: (data, body) => {
      this.context.world.value.destroyBody(body);
    },
  });

  binder = Binder.create<UserData>({
    key: (data) => data.key,
    drivers: [
      Driver.create<BallData, Body>({...}),
      this.railDriver,
      Driver.create<PocketData, Body>({...}),
    ],
  });
```

  handleFrameLoop() {
    const { game } = this.context;
    // The binder calls each driver's update(data, body), which is where the
    // actual physics body is moved. We just pass the current data here.
    this.binder.data([...game.balls, ...game.rails, ...game.pockets]);
  }

  // Move the paddle bodies to match the current keyboard-controlled position.
  // The driver's `update` callback receives the real physics Body (not RailData).
  updateRail(data: RailData, body: Body) {
    if (data.key === "paddle-0") {
      body.setPosition({ x: 0, y: this.clampPaddleY(window.paddle0) });
    } else if (data.key === "paddle-1") {
      body.setPosition({ x: 0, y: this.clampPaddleY(window.paddle1) });
    }
  }

  clampPaddleY(y: number) {
    const table = this.context.game.table;
    // Keep the paddle within the table's vertical extent.
    const limit = table.tableHeight * 0.5 - table.railThickness;
    return Math.max(-limit, Math.min(limit, y));
  }
```

```ts
  handleFrameLoop() {
    const { game } = this.context;
    this.binder.data([...game.balls, ...game.rails, ...game.pockets]);
  }

  // Move a paddle body by dy, clamped to the table's vertical extent.
  // The body itself is the source of truth for the paddle's position.
  movePaddle(key: string, dy: number) {
    const body = this.railDriver.ref(key);
    if (!body) return;

    const table = this.context.game.table;
    const limit = table.tableHeight * 0.5 - table.railThickness;
    const y = Math.max(-limit, Math.min(limit, body.getPosition().y + dy));
    body.setPosition({ x: 0, y });
  }

Done. I removed the `window.paddle0`/`window.paddle1` globals entirely and made the physics body the single source of truth for each paddle's position.

## What changed

**`EightBallGame`** now holds a reference to the physics middleware and tells it to move the paddle directly:
```ts
billiardPhysics = new BilliardPhysics();

// ...
this.use(this.billiardPhysics);

// w / s
this.billiardPhysics.movePaddle("paddle-0", 0.1);
this.billiardPhysics.movePaddle("paddle-0", -0.1);
```

**`BilliardPhysics.movePaddle`** reads the body's *actual current y*, adds the delta, clamps, and writes it back:
```ts
movePaddle(key: string, dy: number) {
  const body = this.railDriver.ref(key);
  if (!body) return;

  const table = this.context.game.table;
  const limit = table.tableHeight * 0.5 - table.railThickness;
  const y = Math.max(-limit, Math.min(limit, body.getPosition().y + dy));
  body.setPosition({ x: 0, y });
}
```

Because the clamp is applied to the body's real position (and the body is the only stored position), there's no hidden counter to unwind anymore. Pressing `w` at the top is a genuine no-op; pressing `s` moves down immediately.

Other cleanup:
- Removed the `window.paddle0` / `window.paddle1` declarations, initialization, and all reads/writes.
- Added `paddleStartY` to `BilliardTableData` so the initial positions live with the table config.
- Kept a reference to the rail `Driver` so `movePaddle` can look up the real `Body` via `railDriver.ref(key)`.
- Removed the old per-frame `updateRail` / `clampPaddleY` logic.

## Verification

- `npx tsc --noEmit` passes.
- `npm run build` passes.
- A planck simulation confirmed: 30 presses of `w` stops at y = 1.80, 5 extra presses at the top leave it at 1.80, and one `s` immediately drops it to 1.70.
- Confirmed `railDriver.ref("paddle-0")` returns the persistent body and `undefined` for unknown keys.

private paddleInputs = new Map<string, { up: boolean; down: boolean; last: "up" | "down" | null }>();

setPaddleKey(key: string, direction: "up" | "down", pressed: boolean) {
  const input = this.paddleInputs.get(key) ?? { up: false, down: false, last: null };
  this.paddleInputs.set(key, input);

  if (pressed) {
    input[direction] = true;
    input.last = direction;

    input[direction] = false;

      // If the other direction is still held, switch to it; otherwise stop.

      input.last = input[other] ? other : null;
    }

  const body = this.railDriver.ref(key);
  if (!body) return;

  const speed = this.paddleSpeed;
  const vy = input.last === "up" ? speed : input.last === "down" ? -speed : 0;
  body.setLinearVelocity({ x: 0, y: vy });
}
```

```ts
updateRail(data: RailData, body: Body) {
  if (data.key !== "paddle-0" && data.key !== "paddle-1") return;

  const table = this.context.game.table;
  const limit = table.tableHeight * 0.5 - table.railThickness;
  const y = body.getPosition().y;
  const vy = body.getLinearVelocity().y;

  if (y <= -limit) {
    if (y < -limit) body.setPosition({ x: 0, y: -limit });
    if (vy < 0) body.setLinearVelocity({ x: 0, y: 0 });
  } else if (y >= limit) {
    if (y > limit) body.setPosition({ x: 0, y: limit });
    if (vy > 0) body.setLinearVelocity({ x: 0, y: 0 });
  }
}
```

}

import { World, Box } from ".../planck.mjs";

const PADDLE_SPEED = 2.0;
const limit = 4.0*0.5 - 0.2; // 1.8

const world = new World({gravity:{x:0,y:0}});
const body = world.createBody({type:"kinematic", position:{x:0,y:0}});
body.createFixture({shape: new Box(0.05,0.19)});

const inputs = new Map();
function setPaddleKey(key, direction, pressed) {
  let input = inputs.get(key);
  if (!input) { input = {up:false,down:false,last:null}; inputs.set(key,input); }
  if (pressed) { input[direction]=true; input.last=direction; }
  else { input[direction]=false; if (input.last===direction) { const other = direction==="up"?"down":"up"; input.last = input[other]?other:null; } }
  const vy = input.last==="up"?PADDLE_SPEED:input.last==="down"?-PADDLE_SPEED:0;
  body.setLinearVelocity({x:0,y:vy});
}
function updateRail() {
  const y = body.getPosition().y;
  const vy = body.getLinearVelocity().y;
  if (y <= -limit) { if (y < -limit) body.setPosition({x:0,y:-limit}); if (vy<0) body.setLinearVelocity({x:0,y:0}); }
  else if (y >= limit) { if (y > limit) body.setPosition({x:0,y:limit}); if (vy>0) body.setLinearVelocity({x:0,y:0}); }
}
function frame() { world.step(1/60); updateRail(); }

## Assistant

_Thinking:_

Everything passes. Let me write the final summary.

Key points:
- Added `PADDLE_SPEED = 2.0` units/sec (tunable).
- `keydown` sets the paddle's velocity via `setPaddleKey`; ignores auto-repeat.
- `keyup` clears the direction and sets velocity to 0 or the opposite held key.
- `paddleInputs` map tracks held up/down and last pressed direction per paddle.
- `updateRail` clamps position at bounds and cancels outward velocity.
- `blur` clears input to avoid stuck movement.
- Both paddles: w/s for left, ArrowUp/ArrowDown for right.
- Verified via simulation and typecheck/build.

Mention that speed can be adjusted via `PADDLE_SPEED`.

I should be concise. Also mention the "intervening keydown of the other direction" behavior: last-pressed direction wins; releasing it falls back to the other held key, otherwise stops.

Let me finalize.

Done. Paddles now move continuously based on velocity instead of per-press position changes.

## What changed

**New constant** (tunable):
```ts
const PADDLE_SPEED = 2.0; // world units per second
```

**`keydown` sets a direction, `keyup` clears it** (auto-repeat is ignored):
```ts
// w / s → paddle-0, ArrowUp / ArrowDown → paddle-1
if (!e.repeat) this.billiardPhysics.setPaddleKey("paddle-0", "up", true);
// keyup:
this.billiardPhysics.setPaddleKey("paddle-0", "up", false);
```

**`BilliardPhysics.setPaddleKey`** tracks the held keys per paddle and sets the body's linear velocity:
```ts
paddleInputs = new Map<
  string,
  { up: boolean; down: boolean; last: "up" | "down" | null }
>();

setPaddleKey(key, direction, pressed) {
  // ...
  const vy =
    input.last === "up" ? PADDLE_SPEED :
    input.last === "down" ? -PADDLE_SPEED : 0;
  body.setLinearVelocity({ x: 0, y: vy });
}
```

Behavior:
- Keydown → velocity is set immediately; the paddle keeps moving until keyup.
- Pressing the opposite direction while the first is held reverses it instantly (last-pressed wins).
- Releasing the active direction falls back to the other still-held key, or stops if neither is held.

**Bound handling** in `updateRail` (runs each frame after the physics step): clamps the body at the table limit and cancels outward velocity, so it can't leave the table and won't accumulate hidden movement.

**Window `blur`** calls `clearPaddleInput()`, so a missed keyup (e.g. alt-tabbing) can't leave a paddle drifting forever.

## Verification

- `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both pass.
- Simulation confirmed: holding up moves at 2.0 units/sec, stops exactly at the 1.8 limit, extra frames don't drift, pressing down reverses immediately, releasing down while up is held resumes up, and releasing all keys stops.

If 2.0 units/sec feels too slow/fast, just adjust `PADDLE_SPEED`.

const CUE_SPEED = 2.0;
let left=0,right=0,minA=Infinity,maxA=-Infinity,minSpeed=Infinity,maxSpeed=-Infinity;
for (let i=0;i<100000;i++){
  const angle = (Math.random()-0.5)*(Math.PI/2);
  const horizontal = Math.random()<0.5?-1:1;
  const vx = horizontal*CUE_SPEED*Math.cos(angle);
  const vy = CUE_SPEED*Math.sin(angle);
  const speed = Math.hypot(vx,vy);
  const deg = Math.atan2(vy, Math.abs(vx))*180/Math.PI; // angle above/below horizontal
  if (horizontal<0) left++; else right++;
  minA=Math.min(minA,deg); maxA=Math.max(maxA,deg);
  minSpeed=Math.min(minSpeed,speed); maxSpeed=Math.max(maxSpeed,speed);
}
console.log({left,right,minAngle:minA,maxAngle:maxA,minSpeed,maxSpeed});
```

## Changes

**New constant** (tunable):
```ts
// Initial cue ball speed in world units per second.
const CUE_SPEED = 2.0;
```

**`BallData`** gained an optional velocity:
```ts
velocity?: { x: number; y: number };
```

**`createBall`** applies it:
```ts
if (data.velocity) {
  body.setLinearVelocity(data.velocity);
}
```

**`cueBall()`** now spawns at center with a random direction:
```ts
cueBall(): BallData {
  // Random direction within 45° above/below horizontal, with left and right
  // equally likely.
  const angle = (Math.random() - 0.5) * (Math.PI / 2); // -45°..45°
  const horizontal = Math.random() < 0.5 ? -1 : 1;

  return {
    type: "ball",
    key: "ball-" + Math.random(),
    position: { x: 0, y: 0 }, // table center == screen center
    velocity: {
      x: horizontal * CUE_SPEED * Math.cos(angle),
      y: CUE_SPEED * Math.sin(angle),
    },
    radius: this.ballRadius,
    color: WHITE,
  };
}
```

Because both the initial rack cue and `resetCueBall()` call `cueBall()`, both the first cue and every respawned cue get the new center spawn and random velocity.

If you want the cue faster or slower, change `CUE_SPEED`.

Let me write:
```ts
setPaddleKey(key: string, direction: "up" | "down", pressed: boolean, speed: number) {
  let input = this.paddleInputs.get(key);
  if (!input) {
    input = { up: false, down: false, last: null };
    this.paddleInputs.set(key, input);
  }

  if (pressed) {
    input[direction] = true;
    input.last = direction;
  } else {
    input[direction] = false;
    if (input.last === direction) {
      const other = direction === "up" ? "down" : "up";
      input.last = input[other] ? other : null;
    }
  }

  this.applyPaddleVelocity(key, speed);
}

// Re-apply the current direction at a new speed (e.g. shift pressed/released
// while a direction key is already held).
setPaddleSpeed(key: string, speed: number) {
  this.applyPaddleVelocity(key, speed);
}

private applyPaddleVelocity(key: string, speed: number) {
  const input = this.paddleInputs.get(key);
  if (!input) return;
  const body = this.railDriver.ref(key);
  if (!body) return;

  const vy =
    input.last === "up" ? speed :
    input.last === "down" ? -speed : 0;
  body.setLinearVelocity({ x: 0, y: vy });
}
```

  } else if (e.code === "ArrowDown") {

    if (!e.repeat) this.billiardPhysics.setPaddleKey("paddle-1", "down", true, this.rightPaddleSpeed());
  }
});
```

	  );
	}
      } else if (e.code === "KeyS") {
	// paddle-0 down
	if (!e.repeat) {
	  this.billiardPhysics.setPaddleKey(
	    "paddle-0", "down", true, this.leftPaddleSpeed(),
	  );
	}
      } else if (e.code === "ArrowUp") {
	// paddle-1 up
	e.preventDefault();
	if (!e.repeat) {
	  this.billiardPhysics.setPaddleKey(
	    "paddle-1", "up", true, this.rightPaddleSpeed(),
	  );
	}
      } else if (e.code === "ArrowDown") {
	// paddle-1 down
	e.preventDefault();
	if (!e.repeat) {
	  this.billiardPhysics.setPaddleKey(
	    "paddle-1", "down", true, this.rightPaddleSpeed(),
	  );
	}
      }
    });

    window.addEventListener("keyup", (e) => {
      if (e.code === "ShiftLeft") {
	this.shiftHeld.left = false;
	this.updatePaddleSpeeds();
	return;
      }
      if (e.code === "ShiftRight") {
	this.shiftHeld.right = false;
	this.updatePaddleSpeeds();
	return;
      }

      if (e.code === "KeyW") {
	this.billiardPhysics.setPaddleKey(
	  "paddle-0", "up", false, this.leftPaddleSpeed(),
	);
      } else if (e.code === "KeyS") {
	this.billiardPhysics.setPaddleKey(
	  "paddle-0", "down", false, this.leftPaddleSpeed(),
	);
      } else if (e.code === "ArrowUp") {
	this.billiardPhysics.setPaddleKey(
	  "paddle-1", "up", false, this.rightPaddleSpeed(),
	);
      } else if (e.code === "ArrowDown") {
	this.billiardPhysics.setPaddleKey(
	  "paddle-1", "down", false, this.rightPaddleSpeed(),
	);
      }
    });

    // Stop any moving paddle if the window loses focus, otherwise a missed
    // keyup could leave it drifting forever.
    window.addEventListener("blur", () => {
      this.shiftHeld.left = false;
      this.shiftHeld.right = false;
      this.billiardPhysics.clearPaddleInput();
    });
  }

  leftPaddleSpeed() {
    return this.shiftHeld.left ? PADDLE_HIGHSPEED : PADDLE_SPEED;
  }

  rightPaddleSpeed() {
    return this.shiftHeld.right ? PADDLE_HIGHSPEED : PADDLE_SPEED;
  }

  updatePaddleSpeeds() {
    this.billiardPhysics.setPaddleSpeed("paddle-0", this.leftPaddleSpeed());
    this.billiardPhysics.setPaddleSpeed("paddle-1", this.rightPaddleSpeed());
  }

Edit 4: Modify `setPaddleKey` to accept `speed` and add `setPaddleSpeed`/`applyPaddleVelocity`.

New:
```ts
  // Update a paddle's held direction. Movement is driven by velocity, so it
  // starts immediately on keydown and continues until keyup (or the other
  // direction key is pressed).
  setPaddleKey(
    key: string,
    direction: "up" | "down",
    pressed: boolean,
    speed: number,
  ) {
    let input = this.paddleInputs.get(key);
    if (!input) {
      input = { up: false, down: false, last: null };
      this.paddleInputs.set(key, input);
    }

    if (pressed) {
      input[direction] = true;
      input.last = direction;
    } else {
      input[direction] = false;
      if (input.last === direction) {
        // Fall back to the opposite key if it is still held, else stop.
        const other = direction === "up" ? "down" : "up";
        input.last = input[other] ? other : null;
      }
    }

    this.applyPaddleVelocity(key, speed);
  }

  // Re-apply the current direction at a new speed, e.g. when Shift is pressed
  // or released while a direction key is already held.
  setPaddleSpeed(key: string, speed: number) {
    this.applyPaddleVelocity(key, speed);
  }

  private applyPaddleVelocity(key: string, speed: number) {
    const input = this.paddleInputs.get(key);
    if (!input) return;

    const body = this.railDriver.ref(key);
    if (!body) return;

    const vy =
      input.last === "up" ? speed :
      input.last === "down" ? -speed : 0;
    body.setLinearVelocity({ x: 0, y: vy });
  }
```

## Changes

**New constant** (I set it to 2× normal — adjust if you want a different value):
```ts
const PADDLE_SPEED = 2.0;
const PADDLE_HIGHSPEED = 4.0;
```

**Key detection switched from `e.key` to `e.code`** for every movement/action key:
```ts
if (e.code === "KeyR") ...
else if (e.code === "KeyC") ...
else if (e.code === "KeyW") ...
else if (e.code === "KeyS") ...
else if (e.code === "ArrowUp") ...
else if (e.code === "ArrowDown") ...
```
`e.code` reports the physical key (`"KeyW"`, `"KeyS"`, `"ArrowUp"`, …) regardless of Shift or Caps Lock, so `"W"`/`"w"` casing no longer matters.

**Left/right Shift are tracked separately:**
```ts
shiftHeld = { left: false, right: false };

leftPaddleSpeed()  { return this.shiftHeld.left  ? PADDLE_HIGHSPEED : PADDLE_SPEED; }
rightPaddleSpeed() { return this.shiftHeld.right ? PADDLE_HIGHSPEED : PADDLE_SPEED; }
```
- Left Shift boosts only `paddle-0` (W/S).
- Right Shift boosts only `paddle-1` (↑/↓).

**Shift changes take effect mid-movement.** Pressing or releasing Shift while a direction key is already held re-applies the current direction at the new speed:
```ts
setPaddleSpeed(key: string, speed: number) {
  this.applyPaddleVelocity(key, speed);
}
```

**Blur** now also clears both Shift flags before stopping the paddles.

## Behavior

| Keys | Result |
|------|--------|
| `w` / `s` | left paddle at 2.0 units/sec |
| `LeftShift` + `w` / `s` | left paddle at 4.0 units/sec |
| `↑` / `↓` | right paddle at 2.0 units/sec |
| `RightShift` + `↑` / `↓` | right paddle at 4.0 units/sec |

Caps Lock and Shift no longer break `w`/`s` (or `r`/`c`) because `e.code` is unaffected by modifiers.

## Verification

- `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` pass.
- A state-machine simulation confirmed: normal → 2.0, Shift → 4.0, Shift release → 2.0, movement key release → 0; left Shift does not boost the right paddle and vice versa; and Shift-before-key works the same as key-before-Shift.

## Assistant

_Thinking:_

The user is asking whether frequent pauses in the action, likely due to garbage collection, can be mitigated by manually triggering garbage collection periodically.

I need to be a helpful programming assistant. Let me think about this carefully.

First, manual GC triggering in JavaScript is generally not possible in browsers. There's no standard API to force GC. `window.gc()` exists only in Firefox with a special flag (`javascript.options.mem.gc_...`?) and in Chrome with `--js-flags="--expose-gc"` for debugging. It's not available in production. So manually triggering GC periodically is not a viable solution.

Second, the pauses are likely not from GC, or if they are, the solution is to reduce allocations in the hot loop, not force GC. In fact, forcing GC more often would likely increase pauses, not reduce them.

Let me investigate the code for allocations in the per-frame loop. The likely culprit is `handleFrameLoop`:
```ts
handleFrameLoop() {
  const { game } = this.context;
  this.binder.data([...game.balls, ...game.rails, ...game.pockets]);
}
```
This creates a new array every frame via spread. With many balls/rails/pockets, that's allocation per frame. Also `Binder.setData` allocates/clears buffers, and may allocate. The `console.log` in keydown only on keypress, not per frame. Are there per-frame logs? We removed the paddle0 log. Let me check.

Also, the physics engine itself allocates during collisions/contacts. Planck might allocate contact objects. The testbed renderer might allocate.

But the user asks specifically: "can this be mitigated by manually triggering garbage collection periodically?" I should answer directly: No, not really—manual GC isn't available in browsers, and forcing it would likely make things worse. The better approach is to reduce allocations in the hot path (the frame loop), which reduces GC pressure and pauses. Then I can implement a fix: avoid the spread array allocation each frame.

Let me examine the code to find per-frame allocations and propose/implement mitigations.

Current `handleFrameLoop`:
```ts
handleFrameLoop() {
  const { game } = this.context;
  this.binder.data([...game.balls, ...game.rails, ...game.pockets]);
}
```

const CONTACT_NORMAL_LENGTH = 0.3;
class DrawContacts extends Middleware {
  constructor() {
    super();
    this.draw = new GLDraw();
    this.manifold = new WorldManifold();
    this.handleFrameRender = () => {
      const world = this.context.world.value;
      if (!world)
        return;
      const renderConfig = this.context.renderConfig.value;
      if (!renderConfig.contact)
        return;
      for (let contact = world.getContactList(); contact; contact = contact.getNext()) {
        if (!contact.isTouching())
          continue;
        const manifold = contact.getWorldManifold(this.manifold);
        if (!manifold)
          continue;
        const normal = manifold.normal;
        for (let i2 = 0; i2 < manifold.pointCount; i2++) {
          const point = manifold.points[i2];
          this.draw.drawScreenPoint(point, 5, "blue");
          if (renderConfig.contactNormals) {
            const p2 = {
              x: point.x + normal.x * CONTACT_NORMAL_LENGTH,
              y: point.y + normal.y * CONTACT_NORMAL_LENGTH
            };
            this.draw.drawEdge(point, p2, "dimgray");
          }
        }
      }
    };
    this.use(this.draw);
    this.on("frame-update", this.handleFrameRender);
  }
}
class WorldStep extends Middleware {

      } catch (error) {
        console.error(error);
        this.context.paused.value = true;
      }

class DrawWorld extends Middleware {
  constructor() {
    super();
    this.draw = new GLDraw();
    this.handleFrameRender = () => {
      const world = this.context.world.value;
      if (!world)
        return;
      if (!this.context.renderConfig.value.shapes)
        return;
      for (let body = world.getBodyList(); body; body = body.getNext()) {
        this.drawBody(body);
      }
    };
    this.use(this.draw);
    this.on("frame-update", this.handleFrameRender);
  }
  drawBody(body) {
    const xf = body.getTransform();
    for (let fixture = body.getFixtureList(); fixture; fixture = fixture.getNext()) {
      const styles = [getStyle(fixture.getShape()), getStyle(fixture), getStyle(body)];
      const color = styles.find((s2) => s2?.stroke)?.stroke ?? shapeColor(body, fixture);
      const fill = styles.find((s2) => s2?.fill)?.fill ?? DEFAULT_FILL;
      const type = fixture.getType();
      if (type === "circle") {
        const shape = fixture.getShape();
        this.draw.drawCircle(shape.m_p, shape.m_radius, color, xf, fill);
      } else if (type === "polygon") {
        const shape = fixture.getShape();
        this.draw.drawPolygon(shape.m_vertices.slice(0, shape.m_count), color, xf, fill);
      } else if (type === "edge") {
        const shape = fixture.getShape();
        this.draw.drawEdge(shape.m_vertex1, shape.m_vertex2, color, xf);
      } else if (type === "chain") {
        const shape = fixture.getShape();
        const vertices = shape.m_vertices;
        const count = shape.m_count;
        for (let i2 = 1; i2 < count; i2++) {
          this.draw.drawEdge(vertices[i2 - 1], vertices[i2], color, xf);
        }
        if (shape.isLoop() && count > 1) {
          this.draw.drawEdge(vertices[count - 1], vertices[0], color, xf);
        }
      }
    }
  }
}
const DEFAULT_FILL = "rgba(255,255,255,0.1)";
const hasStyle = (value) => typeof value === "object" && value !== null && ("stroke" in value || "fill" in value);
function getStyle(obj) {
  if (hasStyle(obj["render"]))
    return obj["render"];
  if (hasStyle(obj["style"]))
    return obj["style"];
}
function shapeColor(body, fixture) {
  if (fixture.isSensor()) {
    return "wheat";
  }
  if (body.isDynamic()) {
    return "rgba(255,255,255,0.9)";
  }
  if (body.isKinematic()) {
    return "rgba(255,255,255,0.8)";
  }
  return "rgba(255,255,255,0.7)";
}

_Thinking:_

`parseColor` caches results in a Map, so it returns the cached array. No per-frame allocation there. `fillColorOf` caches too. Good.

The per-frame allocations in DrawWorld:
- `styles` array per fixture per frame: `[getStyle(shape), getStyle(fixture), getStyle(body)]` — allocates a 3-element array per fixture per frame.
- `styles.find(...)` calls (no allocation beyond the callback? `.find` with arrow function creates a closure per call—yes, the arrow function is created each call, which allocates a function object; though engines may optimize, it's still allocation).
- `shape.m_vertices.slice(0, shape.m_count)` per polygon per frame — allocates a new array.

So for ~20 rail fixtures + 1 ball, that's ~21 arrays for styles + ~20 arrays for slice + ~42 closures per frame = ~80+ allocations/frame, ~4800/sec. That's significant and likely causes GC pauses.

Also, `getStyle` is called 3x per fixture; no allocation there.

  uploadAndDraw(shapeProgram, vao, buffer, data, floatsPerInstance, getCapacity, setCapacity, bindExtra) {
    const instanceCount = data.length / floatsPerInstance;
    if (instanceCount === 0)
      return;
    const gl = this.resources.gl;
    const array = new Float32Array(data);
    gl.bindBuffer(gl.ARRAY_BUFFER, buffer);
    if (array.length > getCapacity()) {
      gl.bufferData(gl.ARRAY_BUFFER, array, gl.DYNAMIC_DRAW);
      setCapacity(array.length);
    } else {
      gl.bufferSubData(gl.ARRAY_BUFFER, 0, array);
    }
    gl.useProgram(shapeProgram.program);
    gl.uniformMatrix4fv(shapeProgram.uProjectionMatrix, false, this.resources.projectionMatrix);
    gl.uniform1f(shapeProgram.uPixelScale, this.resources.pixelScale);
    bindExtra?.();
    gl.bindVertexArray(vao);
    gl.drawArraysInstanced(gl.TRIANGLES, 0, 6, instanceCount);
    gl.bindVertexArray(null);
    gl.useProgram(null);
  }
}

  uploadPolygonVertexTexture() {

    - `uploadPolygonVertexTexture`: was allocating a new `Float32Array` every frame (8KB+); now reuses a grow-only buffer.
    - `uploadAndDraw`: was allocating `new Float32Array(data)` per draw call (3×/frame); now reuses a scratch buffer.
    - `DrawWorld.drawBody`: was allocating a styles array + two `.find` closures + `.slice()` per fixture per frame; now uses direct nullish lookups and a reused points array.
  - `8ball.ts`: `handleFrameLoop` was doing `[...game.balls, ...game.rails, ...game.pockets]` every frame; now reuses a `frameData` array with indexed loops.

Let me write this.

Short answer: **no — manually triggering GC is not a viable fix in a browser**, and it would likely make the pauses worse. Browsers don't expose a standard GC API (`window.gc()` exists only in Chrome with `--js-flags=--expose-gc` or Firefox with a debug pref), and the pause *is* the GC work. Forcing it periodically just schedules more of the same pauses.

The real mitigation is to reduce allocations in the per-frame path. I found and removed the main sources in this project.

## What was allocating every frame

**`vendor/planck/dist/testbed.mjs`** (the testbed renderer):
1. `uploadPolygonVertexTexture()` allocated a **new `Float32Array(width * height * 2)` every frame** — ~8 KB+ per frame for the table geometry.
2. `uploadAndDraw()` allocated `new Float32Array(data)` on **every draw call** (circle, capsule, polygon).
3. `DrawWorld.drawBody()` allocated, **per fixture, per frame**:
   - a `styles` array,
   - two `.find(...)` closures,
   - a `shape.m_vertices.slice(...)` array.

**`8ball.ts`**:
4. `handleFrameLoop()` did `[...game.balls, ...game.rails, ...game.pockets]` every frame — a new array each time.

At 60 fps that was roughly **600 KB/sec of short-lived garbage** for a tiny scene, which explains frequent minor-GC pauses.

## What I changed

- **Game loop** now reuses one array:
  ```ts
  frameData: UserData[] = [];

  handleFrameLoop() {
    const { game } = this.context;
    const data = this.frameData;
    data.length = 0;
    for (let i = 0; i < game.balls.length; i++) data.push(game.balls[i]);
    for (let i = 0; i < game.rails.length; i++) data.push(game.rails[i]);
    for (let i = 0; i < game.pockets.length; i++) data.push(game.pockets[i]);
    this.binder.data(data);
  }
  ```

- **Testbed upload buffers** are now grow-only and reused:
  - `this.polygonRowData` for the vertex texture,
  - `this.uploadArray` for per-draw instance data.

- **`DrawWorld.drawBody`** no longer allocates per fixture:
  ```js
  const color = shapeStyle?.stroke ?? fixtureStyle?.stroke ?? bodyStyle?.stroke ?? shapeColor(body, fixture);
  const fill  = shapeStyle?.fill   ?? fixtureStyle?.fill   ?? bodyStyle?.fill   ?? DEFAULT_FILL;
  ```
  and polygon points are copied into a reused `this.polygonPoints` array instead of `slice()`.

## Verification

- `npx tsc --noEmit` passes.
- `npm run build` passes.
- No behavior changes; only allocation patterns changed.

## Notes

- The testbed fixes are in the vendored file `vendor/planck/dist/testbed.mjs`. If you update/replace `vendor/planck`, those changes will be lost and need reapplying.
- To confirm the improvement, record a few seconds in DevTools **Performance** and look for `Major GC` / `Minor GC` markers, or use the **Memory → Allocation instrumentation on timeline** view. If pauses persist after this, profile again — the next likely source would be contact allocation inside the physics step itself, which is harder to eliminate but usually far less frequent.

import { Middleware } from 'polymatic';
import { Vec2Value, AABBValue, World, Body, Joint, Fixture } from 'planck';
export { Testbed } from 'planck';

/**
 * What a host decides before it runs a project's code: the page it is making, not the commands
 * it sends later. A host writes these while loading, and `initTestbedContext` reads them once,
 * when the testbed mounts — so the project's own `testbed.zoomPan = …`, running afterwards,
 * always wins, with no second writer to race.
 *
 * Changing them after the testbed has mounted does nothing: a host drives a running testbed
 * through its context instead (see `runtime` in api.ts).
 */
interface TestbedDefaults {
    /** zoom with the wheel or a pinch, pan by dragging empty space; off, so an embedded testbed leaves the wheel to the page */
    zoomPan: boolean;
    /** pause after 15 seconds without user input */
    autoPause: boolean;
    /** the view's background color */
    background: string;
--- tail d.ts ---
 * `emit("tool-switch", { name, mouseForce })` picks what the pointer does: "interact-pull",
 * "interact-impulse" (a negative mouseForce throws on release) or "inspect".
 */
declare const TestbedMain: new () => Middleware<TestbedContext>;
/** For games: the testbed's context, with your own state on it. */
declare const initTestbedContext: <C extends object>(state?: C) => TestbedContext & C;
/**
 * For hosts: what to decide before running a project's code — the page you are making, read
 * once when the testbed mounts. The project's own settings run afterwards and win. To drive a
 * testbed that is already running, use `runtime` instead.
 */
declare const testbedDefaults: Partial<TestbedDefaults>;

/** For hosts: mounts the testbed once, as Testbed.mount() does, and returns it. */
declare const mountTestbed: () => TestbedInterface;
/** For hosts: see TestbedRuntime. */
declare const runtime: TestbedRuntime;

export { TestbedMain, initTestbedContext, mountTestbed, runtime, testbedDefaults };
export type { ActiveKeys, FrameCallback, HostContext, HostValue, KeydownCallback, KeyupCallback, StepCallback, StepEvent, Style, TestbedContext, TestbedDefaults, TestbedInterface, TestbedRuntime };

  "path": "/home/tomh/fractional_engineering/8ball/makingpong.md",
  "content": "# Making Pong — 8-Ball Paddle Controls & Performance Work\n\nExport of the OpenCode session working in `/home/tomh/fractional_engineering/8ball`.\n\n---\n\n## Objective\n\nTurn the 8-ball table's side rails into player-controlled paddles:\n\n- **Left paddle** (`paddle-0`) moves with `w` / `s`.\n- **Right paddle** (`paddle-1`) moves with `ArrowUp` / `ArrowDown`.\n- Movement must be **velocity-based and continuous** — start on keydown, continue until keyup, switch direction immediately when the opposite key is pressed.\n- New cue balls spawn at the **table center `(0, 0)`** with a random direction and a fixed speed of **2.0 units/sec**.\n- Later: **Shift-boosted** paddle speeds.\n- Later: mitigate **GC pauses** in the frame loop.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Velocity-based paddle movement\n\n### Design\n\n- The planck `Body` is the **single source of truth** for paddle position. The old `window.paddle0` / `window.paddle1` globals were removed.\n- Paddles are **kinematic** bodies: they stay where driven but still collide with balls.\n- Shape vertices are fixed; only the body's `y` position moves (no baked-in offset).\n- Movement applies `body.setLinearVelocity({ x: 0, y: ±PADDLE_SPEED })`.\n- Bounds are enforced in the rail driver's `update` callback by clamping the body position and zeroing outward velocity, so no hidden accumulated offset can build up.\n\n### Input state\n\nEach paddle tracks its held directions and the most recently pressed one:\n\n```ts\npaddleInputs = new Map<\n  string,\n  { up: boolean; down: boolean; last: \"up\" | \"down\" | null }\n>();\n```\n\n- `last` is the most recently pressed direction, so the opposite key takes over instantly.\n- Releasing `last` falls back to the other key if it is still held, otherwise stops.\n- Auto-repeat `keydown` events are ignored via `!e.repeat`.\n- `blur` clears all paddle input so a missed `keyup` cannot leave a paddle drifting.\n\n### Velocity application\n\n```ts\nsetPaddleKey(\n  key: string,\n  direction: \"up\" | \"down\",\n  pressed: boolean,\n  speed: number,\n) {\n  let input = this.paddleInputs.get(key);\n  if (!input) {\n    input = { up: false, down: false, last: null };\n    this.paddleInputs.set(key, input);\n  }\n\n  if (pressed) {\n    input[direction] = true;\n    input.last = direction;\n  } else {\n    input[direction] = false;\n    if (input.last === direction) {\n      const other = direction === \"up\" ? \"down\" : \"up\";\n      input.last = input[other] ? other : null;\n    }\n  }\n\n  this.applyPaddleVelocity(key, speed);\n}\n\n// Re-apply the current direction at a new speed, e.g. when Shift is pressed\n// or released while a direction key is already held.\nsetPaddleSpeed(key: string, speed: number) {\n  this.applyPaddleVelocity(key, speed);\n}\n\nprivate applyPaddleVelocity(key: string, speed: number) {\n  const input = this.paddleInputs.get(key);\n  if (!input) return;\n\n  const body = this.railDriver.ref(key);\n  if (!body) return;\n\n  const vy =\n    input.last === \"up\" ? speed :\n    input.last === \"down\" ? -speed : 0;\n  body.setLinearVelocity({ x: 0, y: vy });\n}\n```\n\n`BilliardPhysics.railDriver.ref(key)` returns the persistent `Body` for `paddle-0` / `paddle-1`.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Cue ball spawn\n\nNew cue balls now appear at the table center with a random shallow angle:\n\n```ts\n// Initial cue ball speed in world units per second.\nconst CUE_SPEED = 2.0;\n```\n\n- Position: `(0, 0)`.\n- Speed: exactly `2.0` units/sec.\n- Direction: random within ±45° of horizontal, left/right equally likely.\n- Implemented by adding an optional `BallData.velocity` applied in `createBall` via `body.setLinearVelocity`.\n\nVerified over 200k samples: speed exactly 2.0, angle within −45°..45°, left ≈ 50.1%.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Shift-boosted paddle speed\n\n### Problem\n\nPressing Shift (or having Caps Lock on) changed the character reported by `e.key` (`\"w\"` → `\"W\"`), breaking paddle movement.\n\n### Fix: physical key codes\n\nAll key handling switched from `e.key` to **`e.code`**, which is unaffected by Shift and Caps Lock:\n\n```ts\nif (e.code === \"KeyR\") ...\nelse if (e.code === \"KeyC\") ...\nelse if (e.code === \"KeyW\") ...\nelse if (e.code === \"KeyS\") ...\nelse if (e.code === \"ArrowUp\") ...\nelse if (e.code === \"ArrowDown\") ...\n```\n\n### Boost\n\n```ts\n// Paddle movement speed in world units per second.\nconst PADDLE_SPEED = 2.0;\n// Shift-boosted paddle speed in world units per second.\nconst PADDLE_HIGHSPEED = 4.0;\n```\n\nLeft and right Shift are tracked **separately**, so each boosts only its own paddle:\n\n```ts\n// Track Shift keys separately so each one boosts only its own paddle.\nshiftHeld = { left: false, right: false };\n\nleftPaddleSpeed()  { return this.shiftHeld.left  ? PADDLE_HIGHSPEED : PADDLE_SPEED; }\nrightPaddleSpeed() { return this.shiftHeld.right ? PADDLE_HIGHSPEED : PADDLE_SPEED; }\n\nupdatePaddleSpeeds() {\n  this.billiardPhysics.setPaddleSpeed(\"paddle-0\", this.leftPaddleSpeed());\n  this.billiardPhysics.setPaddleSpeed(\"paddle-1\", this.rightPaddleSpeed());\n}\n```\n\n`ShiftLeft` / `ShiftRight` keydown and keyup update `shiftHeld` and call `updatePaddleSpeeds()`, so the change takes effect **mid-movement**.\n\n| Keys | Result |\n|------|--------|\n| `w` / `s` | left paddle at 2.0 units/sec |\n| `LeftShift` + `w` / `s` | left paddle at 4.0 units/sec |\n| `↑` / `↓` | right paddle at 2.0 units/sec |\n| `RightShift` + `↑` / `↓` | right paddle at 4.0 units/sec |\n\n`blur` clears both Shift flags before stopping the paddles.\n\nVerified with a state-machine simulation: normal → 2.0, Shift → 4.0, Shift release → 2.0, key release → 0; left Shift does not boost the right paddle and vice versa; Shift-before-key behaves the same as key-before-Shift.\n\n---\n\n## 4. GC pauses\n\n### Question\n\n> There are somewhat frequent pauses in the action likely due to garbage collection. Can this be mitigated by manually triggering garbage collection periodically?\n\n### Answer\n\nNo. Browsers expose no standard GC API (`window.gc()` only exists in Chrome with `--expose-gc`, or Firefox with a debug pref). Forcing collection just schedules more of the same pauses. The real fix is **reducing per-frame allocations**.\n\n### Sources found\n\nIn `vendor/planck/dist/testbed.mjs`:\n\n1. `uploadPolygonVertexTexture()` allocated a new `Float32Array(width * height * 2)` every frame.\n2. `uploadAndDraw()` allocated `new Float32Array(data)` on every draw call.\n3. `DrawWorld.drawBody()` allocated, per fixture per frame: a `styles` array, two `.find(...)` closures, and a `shape.m_vertices.slice(...)` array.\n\nIn `8ball.ts`:\n\n4. `handleFrameLoop()` built `[...game.balls, ...game.rails, ...game.pockets]` every frame.\n\nAt 60 fps this was roughly 600 KB/sec of short-lived garbage for a tiny scene.\n\n### Fix in the game loop\n\n```ts\n// Reused every frame to avoid allocating a new array in the hot path.\nframeData: UserData[] = [];\n\nhandleFrameLoop() {\n  const { game } = this.context;\n  const data = this.frameData;\n  data.length = 0;\n  for (let i = 0; i < game.balls.length; i++) data.push(game.balls[i]);\n  for (let i = 0; i < game.rails.length; i++) data.push(game.rails[i]);\n  for (let i = 0; i < game.pockets.length; i++) data.push(game.pockets[i]);\n  this.binder.data(data);\n}\n```\n\n### Fixes in the testbed renderer\n\n- Reused grow-only polygon-vertex buffer (`polygonRowData`).\n- Reused grow-only per-draw upload buffer (`uploadArray`).\n- Allocation-free `DrawWorld.drawBody` (direct style lookups + reused `polygonPoints` array instead of `slice()`).\n\nResult: noticeably more playable, with far fewer minor-GC pauses.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Local testbed fork\n\nThe testbed is separate from the planck physics engine. To keep the vendored package pristine, the optimized testbed was moved into the project root.\n\n### File layout\n\n- **`testbed.mjs`** (new) — local fork containing the three allocation reductions, with a header comment documenting the fork.\n- **`testbed.d.ts`** (new) — matching type declarations.\n- **`8ball.ts`** — import changed:\n  ```ts\n  import { initTestbedContext, TestbedMain, type TestbedContext } from \"./testbed\";\n  ```\n- **`vendor/planck/dist/testbed.mjs`** — restored to the original by reversing all four edits.\n\nA `diff` between the vendor file and the local copy shows exactly the intended enhancements, only in the local copy.\n\n---\n\n## Verification\n\n- `npx tsc --noEmit` passes.\n- `npm run build` passes; production bundle includes the local enhanced testbed.\n- Paddle behavior verified with a state-machine simulation (direction switching, fallback, Shift boost, Shift-before-key).\n- Cue direction distribution verified over 200k samples.\n- No per-frame allocation patterns remain in the game hot path (`[...game.*]`, `new Float32Array(data)`, `styles = [`).\n\n---\n\n## Maintenance notes\n\n- The local `testbed.mjs` is a fork of `vendor/planck/dist/testbed.mjs`. Updating the vendored planck/testbed will **not** update the local fork; re-copy and reapply the three allocation fixes if needed.\n- The project is **not** a git repository; all changes are plain file edits.\n- Key files: `8ball.ts`, `testbed.mjs`, `testbed.d.ts`, `vendor/planck/dist/planck.mjs`, `package.json`.\n"
}
```
